prompt
Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_9x9 (precision INT9)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[9], b[9]
  • output ports: out[18]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 18 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 18 bits (modulo 2^18), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

The file tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds. Registers the rewrite adds have no initial value to rely on either.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The rewrite is verified under that assumption.
Check
  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.
  • Every block input port is connected, and the block's outputs drive what the plan says they drive.
  • The correctness rules hold for the edited source.

The deterministic checks have already passed.

Output
  • First line exactly ACCEPT or REJECT.
  • If REJECT: the rule, the lines, what is wrong.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 256
TILES: 80
STAGE_NS: 5.99372
MAPPING: For every one of the 256 seq_mac instances (inside processing_element / processing_element_top_edge / processing_element_bottom_edge, lines 4455 / 4551 / 4648): a = {1'b0, out_a} (the PE's existing 8-bit out_a register, lines 4447/4457-4466, zero-extended to 9); b = {1'b0, out_b[7:0]} (the PE's existing out_b register, low 8 bits, zero-extended to 9); clk = the PE's clk; reset = the PE's reset port, i.e. effective_rst = reset | pe_reset (line 4110); out[15:0] replaces the register out_temp (line 4673) and feeds the unchanged saturating downcast at lines 4707-4719; out[17:16] is left unconnected.

PLAN:

ASSUMES: at every clock edge m at which effective_rst (line 4110) is sampled high and is the last high edge of that assertion, each PE's in_a and in_b[7:0] are already 0 at edges m-1 and m (so the products formed from those two operand pairs are 0). Support: (a) when effective_rst comes from the global reset, held for at least 3 edges, a_mem_access_counter/b_mem_access_counter are cleared (lines 2596-2606) so a_data_valid/b_data_valid are 0 and every aN_data/bN_data is 0 (lines 2609-2647), the skew-delay registers are cleared (lines 2771-2772), and every PE out_a/out_b is cleared (lines 4457-4466), so all PE operands are 0 throughout the assertion; (b) when effective_rst comes from the one-cycle pe_reset pulse (set at line 4886, self-cleared at line 4871), it is issued between matmuls, with start_mat_mul low long enough for zeros to have flushed through the setup pipeline (lines 2575-2606, 2771-2772) and the PE out_a/out_b chain. The original design already depends on the m-1 half of this: its own out_temp adds mul_out_temp_reg — the product of the operands from two edges earlier — on the very first edge after reset (lines 4691-4704), so out_temp(m+1) equals that pre-reset product in the baseline and must be 0 for the baseline matmul to be correct. The rewrite additionally needs the edge-m product to be 0.

1. Candidate regions: what is converted and what is left

Converted: qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac), all 256 instances. This is the only candidate whose arithmetic is what the block computes: qmult (line 4728) forms a_flopped * b_flopped, and qadd (line 4738) adds that product into out_temp, which is a running sum cleared only by a synchronous active-high reset (lines 4697-4704). That is exactly acc += a*b with a synchronous clear and no enable and no load, which is the block's contract.

Left, all for the same reason — the block cannot emit a single product:

  • norm.$mul$...:5070$368/$371/$374/$377(sum_in=top) and norm.$mul$...:5070$380 through $413 (16 products, line 5070).
  • activation.$mul$...:5482$470 through $530 (16 products, line 5482).

Reasons, in detail:

  • The block's out is an accumulator: it adds a term at every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control. There is no enable and no load. Clearing it every cycle cannot be used to turn it into a plain multiplier either, because reset also clears the operand registers, so the term added at the edge after a clear is always 0 — a single, isolated product can never appear on out.
  • Both regions need exactly that: one product captured once into a register that is not a running sum and is conditionally updated. norm's variance_applied_data (line 5070) is written only when in_data_available || norm_in_progress and is otherwise loaded with 0 (lines 5059, 5097-5104), and each slice is muxed against mean_applied_data by validity_mask[i] (lines 5068-5075). activation's slope_applied_data_internal (line 5482) is written only when in_data_available || activation_in_progress and activation_type==1'b1, and simply holds when activation_type==1'b0 (lines 5477-5489), otherwise loaded with 0 (lines 5466-5476, 5521-5531). A hold and a conditional load are not reproducible on a block with no enable and no load path.
  • activation's mult_add adds data_intercept_delayed (line 5485), which is not a product and sits one register stage later; the block's running sum takes no external addend, so the add must stay in soft logic anyway, leaving only a bare multiply to place — which, per the point above, the block cannot deliver.
  • The 12 norm entries marked "products reach no sum" are bare multiplies, which is the case the block cannot do at all.
  • Cost: these 32 multiplies already sit on DSP tiles in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, so converting them would save no tile; and they are all on clk/reset conditions that differ from effective_rst, so they could not share tiles with the 256 MAC blocks in any case.
  • Note on the critical path: the baseline's slowest stage is the norm multiply stage (5.99372 ns), so norm is the only region whose conversion could shorten the clock. It is not legal to convert, as above; and per the baseline data the next stage, in systolic_data_setup, is 5.988 ns, so even a legal norm conversion could buy at most ~0.006 ns. Leaving norm alone costs essentially nothing.

2. Blocks added and tiles used

The rewrite instantiates 256 mac_int_9x9 blocks, one per seq_mac, replacing 256 mult_9x9_fixed_pt instances. All 256 are on the same clock clk and the same reset effective_rst (line 4110 drives every PE, lines 4112-4369), so there is no reset conflict and they pack 4 to a tile: 64 tiles. The 32 remaining mult_9x9_fixed_pt instances (16 in norm, 16 in activation) are untouched; at the baseline's observed density for its partly-filled tiles (44 tiles holding 2 each) they occupy 16 tiles. Total 80 DSP tiles, down from 94 of the device's 132. (If the packer fills those leftovers 4 to a tile the total is 72; 80 is the conservative figure.)

3. Longest series of blocks in any stage

One. No block drives another block: a block's out goes only to the saturating downcast soft logic in seq_mac (lines 4707-4719) and from there into output_logic's registers (lines 2331, 2346). The longest block series is therefore a single block, whose stage is the block's internal path, 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, against the slowest stage of 5.99372 ns.

The three stages the rewrite creates or reshapes, all well under 5.99372 ns:

  • out_a/out_b flip-flop → block input register: 0.060 (soft flip-flop clock-to-output) + 1.314–2.253 (wire, register to first block, measured on this design; median 1.940) + 0.019 (block input setup) = 2.02 ns at the median, 2.33 ns at the sampled maximum. This is routing only, and it is strictly shorter than the baseline stage it replaces (a_flopped → mult_9x9_fixed_pt → mul_out_temp_reg, which carried the same routing plus the multiply).
  • Inside the block: 2.219 ns, stated below the baseline critical path.
  • Block out register → saturating downcast → output_logic register: 0.060 (block output clock-to-Q) + 0.54–1.90 (wire, block into soft logic; median 0.96) + two soft levels for the downcast (the OR-reduce of out_temp[14:7] and the final select, lines 4710-4718) at 0.32–2.91 each (median 1.14) + the c_data_out select and setup ≈ 3.3 ns at the median. The baseline already contained this soft path; the only addition is the DSP-tile-to-soft-logic hop in place of an intra-cluster hop, roughly +0.6 ns on a stage that is not among the design's two slowest.

The slowest stage the rewrite leaves is therefore unchanged: the norm stage at 5.99372 ns, which the rewrite does not touch. STAGE_NS = 5.99372.

4. Soft logic that leaves, and from which stage

Per converted seq_mac, three pieces of soft logic are removed; none of them is in the design's slowest stage (that stage is the norm multiply stage) or in the second slowest (systolic_data_setup):

  • a_flopped and b_flopped (16 flip-flops, lines 4677-4686) leave the stage "PE in_a/in_b → operand flop". They are deleted outright: the PE's existing out_a/out_b registers (lines 4447-4448, 4457-4466) already hold exactly in_a and in_b[7:0] registered on the same edge, and the block is fed from those instead. No register is added anywhere by this rewrite.
  • mul_out_temp_reg (16 flip-flops, lines 4681, 4691-4693) leaves the stage "a_flopped → multiply → product flop". Its place in the pipeline is taken by the block's own operand register, which registers a/b at the same edge; the multiply moves from mult_9x9_fixed_pt into the block.
  • qadd, the 16-bit soft adder (line 4738), together with the 16 out_temp flip-flops and the 16-bit feedback net (lines 4673, 4697-4704), leaves the stage "product flop → adder → accumulator flop". That whole stage disappears into the block's running sum.

That is 48 flip-flops and one 16-bit adder removed per PE, 12288 flip-flops and 256 adders across the array, plus the 16-bit DSP-output net and the 16-bit accumulator feedback net per PE — the dominant soft-logic and wirelength cost in this design. Against that, two small items are added back: the out_a registers of column 15 and the out_b registers of row 15 were dead in the baseline and are now read by their own PE's block, so 256 flip-flops (16 PEs × 8 bits, twice) return. Net effect on the objective: delay unchanged at 5.99372 ns, DSP tile area down by 14 × 253779 ≈ 3.6e6, cluster area and routed wirelength both down substantially (on the order of 30-45% of the baseline's 6.2834e7 total area and 395418 wirelength), so area × delay × wirelength falls well below the baseline 1.48918e14.

5. Register stages from operands to result, for the converted region

Before: 3 stages from a PE's in_a/in_b to out_temp — a_flopped/b_flopped (edge k), mul_out_temp_reg (edge k+1), out_temp (edge k+2). Operands present before edge k are added into out_temp at edge k+2.

After: 3 stages — out_a/out_b (edge k, existing PE registers), the block's operand register (edge k+1), the block's running-sum register out (edge k+2). Operands present before edge k are added into out at edge k+2. Latency and cycle-by-cycle value are identical: out[15:0] equals the baseline out_temp on every cycle, because each 8×8 unsigned product is exact in 16 bits (qmult operands are unsigned at line 4724-4728, the block zero-extends 8 to 9 and is unsigned, max 65025 < 2^16) and the low 16 bits of a sum taken mod 2^18 are the sum taken mod 2^16, which is what out_temp holds. out[17:16] is discarded.

6. Editor instructions

  • Add a mac_int_9x9 instance inside seq_mac: mac_int_9x9 u_mac_hard(.clk(clk), .reset(reset), .a({1'b0, a}), .b({1'b0, b}), .out(mac_out)); with wire [17:0] mac_out;.
  • Delete from seq_mac: a_flopped, b_flopped and their always block (lines 4677-4686); the qmult instance and mul_out_temp/mul_out_temp_reg (lines 4680-4681, 4689-4693); the qadd instance and add_out (lines 4675, 4697); the out_temp register and its always block (lines 4673, 4699-4704). Also the unused mul_out wire (line 4674).
  • Replace out_temp in the downcast expression (lines 4707-4719) with mac_out[2*\DWIDTH-1:0], i.e. mac_out[15:0]; keep that expression otherwise byte-for-byte, including its out_temp[2*\DWIDTH-1] sign test, since it is the only reader of the accumulator and must stay outside the block.
  • In processing_element, processing_element_top_edge and processing_element_bottom_edge, change the seq_mac instantiation (lines 4455, 4551, 4648) from .a(in_a), .b(in_b[\DWIDTH-1:0]) to .a(out_a), .b(out_b[\DWIDTH-1:0]). Leave the out_a/out_b always blocks (lines 4457-4466 and their counterparts) exactly as they are — they are now read by the MAC as well as by the neighbouring PE.
  • Leave seq_mac's port list, processing_element's ports, effective_rst (line 4110) and every PE instantiation (lines 4112-4369) unchanged. qmult and qadd become unreferenced; they may be left in the file.
  • Do not touch norm, activation, systolic_data_setup, output_logic, control, cfg, pool or the RAMs.

prompt

4 paragraph breaks restored by the export, where the recorder joined text blocks with no separator.

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_9x9 (precision INT9)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[9], b[9]
  • output ports: out[18]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 18 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 18 bits (modulo 2^18), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified tpu_like.small.os.fixed under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, seed 1) has a worst register-to-register path of 5.99372 ns on its clock clk (VPR's intra-domain critical path, the worst over its clocks clk, clk_mem) and uses 94 DSP tiles of the device's 132. Its used logic-block area is 6.2834e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905) and its routed wirelength is 395418, so its area x delay x wirelength product, the objective, is 1.48918e+14. Its slowest stage is in norm: one hard multiply on a DSP tile (mult_9x9_fixed_pt), then one LUT, into a flip-flop with synchronous reset; wire 1.83 ns from the register into the multiply, 1.77 from the multiply into the LUT. The next slowest stage, in systolic_data_setup, is no multiply, a register through five LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset; wire 5.07 ns in all, 2.12 of it into the last LUT, 5.988 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

A row marked "on this design" is measured on the unmodified design's own placement under this run's VPR settings, with the design's hard multiplier standing in for the block; the other rows are sampled over earlier runs of other designs on this architecture.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, what it already is in the baseline: on a DSP tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, four to a tile on 50 of the 94 tiles (the 256 element products among them; the split by family is not on file).

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds. Registers the rewrite adds have no initial value to rely on either.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The rewrite is verified under that assumption.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when soft adders and flip-flops move into blocks.
  • A DSP tile holds four blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile. Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
Objective

Minimize area × delay × wirelength against the baseline. If you expect no rewrite to lower it, output TIER: NONE. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: No rewrite: no candidate can be rewritten correctly.
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, one integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, in ns, one number>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • A reviewer checks the plan automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.

On this architecture one DSP tile costs 253779 and one CLB 27905; mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode packs four blocks to a tile, so a block that does not shorten the slowest stage is paid for at about 2.3 CLBs when it shares a tile and about 9 when it does not (the blocks of a tile share one reset and one clock).

response

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: NONE
REGIONS: NONE
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 0
TILES: 94
STAGE_NS: 5.99372
MAPPING: NONE — this plan instantiates no block; the measured kept candidate (256 mac_int_9x9, one per seq_mac, a={1'b0,out_a}, b={1'b0,out_b[7:0]}, clk=clk, reset=effective_rst, out[15:0]→out_temp) remains the best result available and is kept.
PLAN:
I judge the kept first-pass rewrite (qmult/seq_mac, 256 blocks, product 7.08066e+13, -52.45% vs baseline) to be the best result this block can reach on this design, and propose no revision. Header figures above describe the no-further-rewrite case (the unmodified source: 94 DSP tiles, slowest stage 5.99372 ns); the kept candidate is retained as the winner.

Candidate-by-candidate disposition.

  1. qmult.$mul$...4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac), 256 instances — already converted in the kept candidate and nothing further is available from it. Register stages, before: in_a/in_b → a_flopped/b_flopped (edge k), → mul_out_temp_reg (edge k+1), → out_temp (edge k+2): 3 stages from the PE's inputs to the accumulated result. After: in_a/in_b → PE out_a/out_b (edge k), → the block's own operand register (edge k+1), → the block's running sum out (edge k+2): still 3 stages, same value on the same cycle, so behaviour is unchanged (a_flopped was a duplicate of out_a; b_flopped[7:0] of out_b[7:0]). Soft logic that left those stages: per PE, the 8+8-bit operand flops, the 16-bit product register, the 16-bit qadd adder and the 16-bit out_temp register — about 2 CLBs per PE, which is where the -22.9% area and -33.2% wirelength came from. The longest series of blocks in any stage is one (out_a/out_b flop → wire → block input register, and block out → the unchanged saturating downcast → out_c); no two blocks are in series anywhere, so the in-block 2.219 ns path is not near the period. The stage this rewrite touches is not the design's slowest stage either before or after: the measured period of 5.53734 ns at seed 1 runs from a RAM output through a mux, three carry-chain adders and two LUTs into $sdffe~3001^Q~3, which no candidate in the list touches. Re-emitting this same conversion would reproduce the same design, not lower the product, so there is nothing to revise here. For the record, the first-pass header understated tile count: 256 blocks plus the 32 remaining soft multiplies packed to 95 tiles, not 80; the packer does not fill every tile with four blocks (the baseline shows the same, 50 of 94 tiles with four multiplies), and nothing in a plan controls that — all 256 blocks already share one clock and one reset (effective_rst, line 4110), so reset grouping is not the limit.
  2. norm.$mul$...5070$380 … $413 (12 instances, "multiply", no sum) and norm.$mul$...5070$368/$371/$374/$377(sum_in=top) (4 instances, sop) — left. variance_applied_data[i] <= mean_applied_data[i] * inv_var (line 5070) is written on every clock edge the norm pipeline is running, and each write must be exactly one product, with a new column of data arriving each cycle. mac_int_9x9 is accumulate-only: out is a running sum that adds a term at every edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load). To get a single product per cycle out of it the block would have to be cleared on alternate edges, which halves throughput and changes the design's cycle-by-cycle output, or the previous accumulator value would have to be subtracted outside (a 16-bit register plus a 16-bit subtractor per instance, and an extra stage after the block). Both lose: the multiply already sits on a DSP tile, so no tile is saved, and the added soft subtract/register raises area, wirelength and the stage delay. Also, these registers are cleared not only by reset but whenever ~enable_norm or the pipeline is idle (lines 5050, 5097-5104), a clear condition the block's single synchronous reset can reproduce but only together with the per-cycle clearing problem above, which is the blocking one. The four sum_in=top instances fail for the same reason, and additionally their sum is a combinational chain of four distinct products in one cycle, not a sum over time, which is the only kind of sum this block performs.
  3. activation.$mul$...5482$470 … $530 (16 instances, mult_add) — left, for the same reason. Line 5482 writes slope_applied_data_internal[i] <= data_slope_flopped[i] * inp_data_flopped[i] on every active edge: one product per cycle, not a running sum. The + data_intercept_delayed[i] of line 5485 is a separate register stage and not a product, so it has no matching block port and would stay in soft logic anyway. The same clearing conditions as norm apply (lines 5466, 5521-5531). As with norm, these 16 multiplies already occupy DSP tiles, so conversion could not save a tile even if it were correct.

So: 32 of the 33 candidate rows (all of norm and all of activation) cannot be rewritten onto an accumulate-only block without changing what the design computes or without soft compensation logic that costs more than it saves, and the one row that can — the 256 seq_mac products — is already converted in the kept candidate. No revision is proposed; this plan adds 0 blocks, leaves the design at its baseline 94 DSP tiles and its 5.99372 ns slowest stage, moves no soft logic out of any stage, and puts no blocks in series, and the kept first-pass result (95 tiles, 5.53734 ns, 4.84379e+07 area, 263990 wirelength, 7.08066e+13) stands.

5042	//-: indicates the part-select decreases from the starting point

5062	        //We have a pipeline here. First stage does the add (to apply the mean)
5063	        //and second stage does the multiplication (to apply the variance).
5064	        //Note: the following loop is not a loop across multiple columns of data.

5074	                variance_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] <= (mean_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH]);
5075	            end
5076	        end

5079	        //in the first cycle, we only apply the mean.

Equivalence checks recorded on this run

Every check written into the record by verify-record, oldest first, not only the latest per candidate: a re-recording keeps the entry it replaces under verification_history. The reference is what the candidate was compared with (the run's own pristine design unless the driver named another); the boundary is the module the checks compared at; the testbench is the stimulus the simulation ran (a candidate checked with more than one testbench has an entry per testbench, the verdict one under the candidate's own label and any other under <label>@<testbench>). Objective loop

1 rewrite(s) measured · stopped by: planner declined
revisions allowed
5
seeds per rewrite

Pass 1
loop stopped by
planner declined (no representative mapping)
objective
area x delay x wirelength (delay register to register, 2026-10-20)
baseline
layout koios_extra_small · used area 6.2834e+07 · critical path 5.99372 ns · wirelength 395418 · area x delay x wirelength (delay register to register, 2026-10-20) 1.48918e+14 (read from entry)

The objective is area x delay x wirelength (delay register to register, 2026-10-20): VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded) times the critical path times the total routed wirelength. Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the product is the product of the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 3.76%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 3.76%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 5 revisions have been made.

Pass 2not approved: planner_declined — planner declined: no representative mapping

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.

On this design, placement seeds alone move the unmodified design's area x delay x wirelength product by about 3.8%; a product change inside that is not evidence of improvement.

Area is VPR's "Total used logic block area" in minimum-width transistor areas; wirelength is
VPR's total routed wirelength. On this architecture one DSP tile costs 253779 and one CLB 27905; mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode packs four blocks to a tile, so a block that does not shorten the slowest stage is paid for at about 2.3 CLBs when it shares a tile and about 9 when it does not (the blocks of a tile share one reset and one clock).

The plan, in full 5526 chars · not approved

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: NONE
REGIONS: NONE
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 0
TILES: 94
STAGE_NS: 5.99372
MAPPING: NONE — this plan instantiates no block; the measured kept candidate (256 mac_int_9x9, one per seq_mac, a={1'b0,out_a}, b={1'b0,out_b[7:0]}, clk=clk, reset=effective_rst, out[15:0]→out_temp) remains the best result available and is kept.
PLAN:
I judge the kept first-pass rewrite (qmult/seq_mac, 256 blocks, product 7.08066e+13, -52.45% vs baseline) to be the best result this block can reach on this design, and propose no revision. Header figures above describe the no-further-rewrite case (the unmodified source: 94 DSP tiles, slowest stage 5.99372 ns); the kept candidate is retained as the winner.

Candidate-by-candidate disposition.

  1. qmult.$mul$...4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac), 256 instances — already converted in the kept candidate and nothing further is available from it. Register stages, before: in_a/in_b → a_flopped/b_flopped (edge k), → mul_out_temp_reg (edge k+1), → out_temp (edge k+2): 3 stages from the PE's inputs to the accumulated result. After: in_a/in_b → PE out_a/out_b (edge k), → the block's own operand register (edge k+1), → the block's running sum out (edge k+2): still 3 stages, same value on the same cycle, so behaviour is unchanged (a_flopped was a duplicate of out_a; b_flopped[7:0] of out_b[7:0]). Soft logic that left those stages: per PE, the 8+8-bit operand flops, the 16-bit product register, the 16-bit qadd adder and the 16-bit out_temp register — about 2 CLBs per PE, which is where the -22.9% area and -33.2% wirelength came from. The longest series of blocks in any stage is one (out_a/out_b flop → wire → block input register, and block out → the unchanged saturating downcast → out_c); no two blocks are in series anywhere, so the in-block 2.219 ns path is not near the period. The stage this rewrite touches is not the design's slowest stage either before or after: the measured period of 5.53734 ns at seed 1 runs from a RAM output through a mux, three carry-chain adders and two LUTs into $sdffe~3001^Q~3, which no candidate in the list touches. Re-emitting this same conversion would reproduce the same design, not lower the product, so there is nothing to revise here. For the record, the first-pass header understated tile count: 256 blocks plus the 32 remaining soft multiplies packed to 95 tiles, not 80; the packer does not fill every tile with four blocks (the baseline shows the same, 50 of 94 tiles with four multiplies), and nothing in a plan controls that — all 256 blocks already share one clock and one reset (effective_rst, line 4110), so reset grouping is not the limit.
  2. norm.$mul$...5070$380 … $413 (12 instances, "multiply", no sum) and norm.$mul$...5070$368/$371/$374/$377(sum_in=top) (4 instances, sop) — left. variance_applied_data[i] <= mean_applied_data[i] * inv_var (line 5070) is written on every clock edge the norm pipeline is running, and each write must be exactly one product, with a new column of data arriving each cycle. mac_int_9x9 is accumulate-only: out is a running sum that adds a term at every edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load). To get a single product per cycle out of it the block would have to be cleared on alternate edges, which halves throughput and changes the design's cycle-by-cycle output, or the previous accumulator value would have to be subtracted outside (a 16-bit register plus a 16-bit subtractor per instance, and an extra stage after the block). Both lose: the multiply already sits on a DSP tile, so no tile is saved, and the added soft subtract/register raises area, wirelength and the stage delay. Also, these registers are cleared not only by reset but whenever ~enable_norm or the pipeline is idle (lines 5050, 5097-5104), a clear condition the block's single synchronous reset can reproduce but only together with the per-cycle clearing problem above, which is the blocking one. The four sum_in=top instances fail for the same reason, and additionally their sum is a combinational chain of four distinct products in one cycle, not a sum over time, which is the only kind of sum this block performs.
  3. activation.$mul$...5482$470 … $530 (16 instances, mult_add) — left, for the same reason. Line 5482 writes slope_applied_data_internal[i] <= data_slope_flopped[i] * inp_data_flopped[i] on every active edge: one product per cycle, not a running sum. The + data_intercept_delayed[i] of line 5485 is a separate register stage and not a product, so it has no matching block port and would stay in soft logic anyway. The same clearing conditions as norm apply (lines 5466, 5521-5531). As with norm, these 16 multiplies already occupy DSP tiles, so conversion could not save a tile even if it were correct.

So: 32 of the 33 candidate rows (all of norm and all of activation) cannot be rewritten onto an accumulate-only block without changing what the design computes or without soft compensation logic that costs more than it saves, and the one row that can — the 256 seq_mac products — is already converted in the kept candidate. No revision is proposed; this plan adds 0 blocks, leaves the design at its baseline 94 DSP tiles and its 5.99372 ns slowest stage, moves no soft logic out of any stage, and puts no blocks in series, and the kept first-pass result (95 tiles, 5.53734 ns, 4.84379e+07 area, 263990 wirelength, 7.08066e+13) stands.
